# Phase 6 â€” presentation, localization and remade assets

**Outcome:** all nine Cosmic Forge source tabs remain clear and usable as MIAPLACIDUS, with a new tenth top-level Miaplaedia guide/reference tab, on desktop and responsive mobile browsers. This work runs alongside the mechanics phases and expands [M-06](../master-checklist.md). Use the nine source tab renderers, `ui.js`, `descriptions.js`, `localization.json`, theme styles, onboarding, audio and presentation test areas as the reference. All visual assets are newly made; reuse the 30 source MP3s unchanged under the owner's explicit 2026-10-04 authorization and record their source and usage.

Rough per-item completion estimates and a closed-item reference are tracked in [phase-6-estimations.md](phase-6-estimations.md).

**P-44 Cosmic Rip closure result dialog and ending (12 October 2026):** the accepted cosmic-rip.closed event queues the six-locale confirm-only source modal; only explicit confirmation after queued result dialogs drain starts the new 22-second React ending sequence. **P-56 performance follow-up (9 October):** The focused Chrome 153 profile on the late-game navigation fixture captured the exact `#tab-settings` target, 0.1ms to the next microtask, zero Long Animation Frames and Long Tasks over five seconds, and 13.78ms inclusive in two sampled `tick` calls. The store now skips a redundant input-state validation on store-owned transitions while retaining full validation of every result and strict validation in the public `transition()` API. The repeat after this change also had no Long Animation Frame or Long Task; its five-second sample recorded 12.3ms inclusive in eight `tick` samples and 3.32ms in `isValidGameState`. Neither profile reproduced the owner-reported 2,032ms frame, whose `DIV#root.onclick` entry lacks the clicked element and expanded `tick` call trees. The separate 1,276ms `DIV#root.onsubmit` is the Pioneer start form. P-56 remains open for an attributable live trace or owner confirmation; see the [performance report](../../../tests/e2e/performance/README.md).

## Navigation and screen coverage

- [x] **P-01** Inventory every pane, button, modal, side menu, tooltip, alert and locked state in the nine source tabs. Evidence: the page map is in [the presentation navigation contract](../presentation-navigation-contract.md); pane actions, values, gates and feedback are inventoried in the linked [source contract](../../audit/foundation-source-contract.md) and economy, space, and meta catalogues.
- [x] **P-02** Define stable route/panel/action IDs independent of translated display strings. Evidence: source tab IDs plus the appended Miaplaedia ID in `GAME_TABS`; Resources, Energy, Research, Compounds, Interstellar, Space Mining, Galactic, Cosmic Rip, Settings, and Miaplaedia child IDs in `presentationNavigation.ts`; and stable command discriminants in `engine/commands.ts`.
- [x] **P-03** build ten semantic top-level tab views in owner-updated order: Resources, Compounds (second whenever unlocked), Research, Energy, Space Mining, Interstellar, Galactic, Cosmic Rip, Settings, then Miaplaedia. Evidence: `GAME_TABS` and semantic `tab`/`tabpanel` markup in `src/app/App.tsx`; tab labels use names only; Galactic Casino stays under Galactic, Star Data stays under Interstellar, and the seven source Cosmicopedia documents live under Miaplaedia; see [the presentation navigation contract](../presentation-navigation-contract.md).
- [x] **P-04** Preserve the owner-updated tab order without renumbering internal source positions, hidden child-page gates, attention indicators and navigation history. Compounds follows Resources whenever unlocked; other tabs retain their declaration order, locked tabs are omitted, and Miaplaedia follows Settings (see the [navigation contract](../presentation-navigation-contract.md#source-to-remake-screen-map)). Locked child pages stay hidden, including each Rocket N pane until the launch pad and that rocket are built; the Launch Pad page keeps build cards available, and one rocket build reveals only its own Rocket pane. Schema v44 persists pending destination markers per run and visited destination IDs save-wide. Initial available pages are marked once; new destinations receive a badge only if never visited; opening a page clears its pending badge and records the visit; both histories survive rebirth. The v43-to-v44 migration preserves pending badges; because v43 did not save cleared-page history, resume marks currently available nonpending pages as visited to preserve visible cleared pages best-effort. Previously visited pages that were hidden when an older save was created cannot be distinguished from first-time unlocks. Settings and Miaplaedia use the same markers. The Black Hole charge-ready badge remains on its Galactic pane and parent tab until warp starts. Galactic Casino keeps its third Galactic-child position after Rebirth and Market; its page and actions follow the synthetic source marker added on first-run Starship arrival and seeded again on rebirth, before the actual current-run AP award. The CP status-row follows the same Galactic root gate. The former 35/35 and 1/1 results exercised the superseded AP-award target gate; the arrival/rebirth assertions were updated on 12 October but were not rerun. ## News Ticker source parity

The News Ticker is the scrolling strip directly below the game header, not a tab. It is enabled by default and can be hidden or restored in **Settings â†’ Game Options â†’ News Ticker**. If it is absent in a playthrough, check that saved toggle first. Keep its behavior aligned with Cosmic Forge (`cosmicForge/cosmicForge/game.js`, `ui.js`, and `descriptions.js`): rotate messages on the source's randomized 20â€“35 second interval; use the source mix of 3% one-off rewards, 10% resource prizes, 15% wacky prompts, and ordinary headlines otherwise; on ordinary headlines, offer an eligible manuscript clue 25% of the time. Prize headlines must show the exact claimable amount and the inline `here` action must award that amount; after claiming, keep the word `here`, disable further clicks, and show it at 50% opacity instead of replacing it with a claimed label. Type-e star bonuses affect resource autobuyer production, not ticker prize amounts. One-off items must only be claimable once. Manuscript clues must refer to eligible, unreported manuscripts and record which clues were shown. All eight source wacky prompts (wave, disco, bounce, fade, glitch, wobble, boo, feedback) must be clickable and run their matching effect until the next message replaces it; preserve seen, claimed, clue, and activated state for the Events history and related statistics/achievements. The source feedback prompt offers separate positive and negative choices; use those local visual responses without adding feedback transmission or analytics. Keep the source headline copy in all six locales with the requested Miaplacidus/Mia'Plac names.

**Verification links:** **P-40** owns the saved toggle; **P-54** must cover a focused `news-ticker` browser area for visibility, timing/category selection, prize and one-off claims, manuscript clues, each wacky effect, keyboard access, and toggle persistence; **P-56** covers responsive and theme presentation. **P-58** tracks the implementation and remaining review.

**P-56 static Settings/Miaplaedia render follow-up (8 October):** Visual Options and Game Options now skip rerenders when only run state changes; locale/settings and navigation-attention updates still refresh them. Miaplaedia articles memoize by locale and document ID, avoiding repeated paragraph parsing on simulation publications. A repeat isolated Chrome profile recorded a 51.4ms maximum LoAF, 30.2ms `tick` callback, and no 50ms Long Tasks in five seconds after selecting Settings; Miaplaedia had no LoAF or Long Task in its one-second sample. Focused Chrome profile passed 1/1, Settings/Miaplaedia layout checks 5/5, Miaplaedia six-locale matrix 1/1, and TypeScript/formatting passed. These single-run measurements do not demonstrate a before/after speedup or explain the owner-reported 2,032ms event. The CUA runtime could not attach to the owner's existing browser; a trace that includes the target element and expanded `tick` stacks is still needed to attribute that stall. P-56 stays open. Details are in the [performance report](../../../tests/e2e/performance/README.md).

**P-56 foreground catch-up and navigation work follow-up (8 October):** the supplied 2,032ms LoAF listed two `tick` callbacks around a 951ms root click dispatch, while prior profiles showed that the live loop recalculated `createEconomyTickPlan` on every animation frame and let one callback process four 250ms catch-up slices. `App.tsx` now runs that engine work at the 250ms display cadence and sets a one-slice budget for live and visibility-change ticks; if a long frame leaves time pending, later frames continue processing it. This preserves the elapsed-time queue. Navigation attention for a parent tab and its selected page now clears in one engine transition. The focused Chrome profile passed 1/1: repeat Settings captures measured maximum LoAFs of 53.7ms and 58.2ms, with `tick` scripts of 29.6ms and 58.1ms; one run had a 58ms Long Task, the other none. The owner-reported two-second frame remains unreproduced and the maximum LoAF is not lower than the prior 51.4ms sample, so this is a bounded-work fix rather than a confirmed end-to-end speedup. The five-second sample attributed 4.56ms inclusive CPU-sampled time to `tick` across three samples. Focused clock/navigation units passed 27/27 and TypeScript passed. Full limits and method are in the [performance report](../../../tests/e2e/performance/README.md); P-56 remains open for owner confirmation and broader presentation review.

**P-56 owner trace interpretation:** the supplied first frame is a start-form `onsubmit`, not a Settings click. The second frame names a root click but omits the clicked element; its main-thread script attribution includes a React click dispatch and two animation-frame callbacks. Neither report attributes the delay to Paint or SVG raster work. The isolated Settings profile is still the closest reproducible comparison, but its 53.7â€“58.2ms maxima do not confirm a fix for the owner's ~2s interaction. P-56 stays open until the same click is recorded with its target and expanded `tick` call stacks.

The all-theme matrix passed 1/1 and the six-locale matrix passed 1/1 in installed Chrome. The German 320px case exposed the Achievements count badge overflowing its heading; the badge now wraps below the heading at narrow widths. The checks assert localized identity, Hydrogen title scale, the three journal pages' no-artwork rule, and viewport bounds. See the [theme-presentation README](../../../tests/e2e/theme-presentation/README.md).

**P-56 Settings profile repeat (8 October):** the focused isolated Chrome profile was rerun against the `late-game-navigation` fixture at 1280x720. It recorded the actual `#tab-settings` click, 0.1ms to the next microtask, no Long Animation Frame, no Long Task, and no `tick` LoAF script in the following five seconds. **P-56 queued-clock correctness follow-up (8 October):** bounded catch-up now saves each queued foreground interval with its assigned multiplier, so delayed slices retain finite-warp and always-on Black Hole rates after later timer expiry. Pause keeps already accrued queued time and paused frames update the wall sample without accruing more foreground time. Local and portable saves retain the queue; save writes advance the saved wall sample to their `savedAt` value to prevent queued foreground time from being miscounted as offline time on resume. Save schema 45 migrates v44 queued duration at normal rate because v44 did not record the original rate. Focused clock/save coverage passed 63/63, TypeScript passed, and the repeated Settings profile passed 1/1 with no LoAF or Long Task. P-56 remains open because the owner's original ~2s click has not been reproduced in this isolated run.

**P-56 hidden economy rail follow-up (8 October):** the navigation trace showed the hidden resource and compound rails rebuilding their `EconomicGoodEmblem` children while Settings/Miaplaedia was selected. `FreezeInactiveEconomyRail` now lets a rail commit its hidden state, then skips updates while it stays hidden; switching back renders it with current quantities and settings. The focused regression confirms the hidden Hydrogen rail stays unchanged during a 300,000ms simulation advance and refreshes when Hydrogen is selected again. Together with the Settings profile, the focused Chrome run passed 2/2. That profile recorded a 0.1ms click-to-microtask interval, no LoAF or Long Task in the five-second Settings window, and 8.5% sampled active CPU; this single sample is not a strict before/after performance claim. The owner-supplied 2,032ms root click still lacks a clicked-element ID and expanded `tick` stacks, so this removes a measured background-render path but does not sign off the reported stall. P-56 remains open. See the [performance report](../../../tests/e2e/performance/README.md).

**P-56 economy-plan and transition-validation follow-up (8 October):** `createEconomyTickPlan` is now memoized by immutable `GameState` identity so snapshot selection and status-bar rendering reuse the current plan; each changed state still recalculates it. `transition()` retains full input and final post-achievement validation but no longer validates each intermediate `success()` result. Two focused Chrome repeats after these changes sampled `isValidGameState` at 15.06ms and 15.02ms inclusive, compared with 48.05ms before; sampled `tick` totals were 17.17ms and 27.53ms, compared with 32.64ms before. Both repeats recorded no LoAF or Long Task, compared with a 99.6ms maximum LoAF and two Long Tasks before. Economy/engine unit tests passed 41/41 and TypeScript passed. The isolated test-mode captures do not reproduce the owner's ~2s frame; P-56 remains open. See the [performance report](../../../tests/e2e/performance/README.md).

**P-56 start-form save lookup follow-up (8 October):** the `1,276ms` `DIV#root.onsubmit` script is the Pioneer start form. Its duplicate-name lookup repeated a full local-save index rebuild, which enumerated storage and decoded every save generation. The repository now reuses a validated per-instance index snapshot until storage or the index changes, invalidates it on cross-tab storage events, and updates it incrementally after commits while retaining recovery generations. Focused local-save tests passed 53/53, including cache reuse and cross-repository invalidation; TypeScript passed. The repeated Settings/Miaplaedia Chrome profile passed 1/1 with a `0.1ms` Settings click-to-microtask measurement and no Long Animation Frame or Long Task in the following five seconds. That profile clears local storage, so it does not provide a before/after duration for the ownerâ€™s accumulated-save submit. The separate `2,032ms` root click still lacks a target ID and expanded `tick` stacks; P-56 remains open for that attribution or owner confirmation. Full evidence and limits are in the [performance report](../../../tests/e2e/performance/README.md).

**P-56 hidden-tab element construction follow-up (9 October):** the Settings profile now visits every unlocked top-level tab before measuring Settings. The matched all-tabs-mounted test-mode sample showed 313.46ms of React JSX factory self time and 267.52ms inclusive in `GameSession` before the change. `FreezeInactiveTabContent` now defers rendering through a callback, so inactive visited panes retain their prior subtree without rebuilding their JSX on parent renders. The repeat sampled 182.53ms in the JSX factory (42% lower) and 121.12ms inclusive in `GameSession` (55% lower); it recorded a 0.2ms click-to-microtask interval and no Long Animation Frame, Long Task, or `tick` LoAF script over five seconds. **P-29 catch-up battle audio queue (12 October 2026, superseded later the same day):** each continuing `space.battle.round` event queued one randomized laser proxy; the engine emitted no round event for the terminal round, and `space.battle.finished` cleared pending proxies without adding a terminal laser. Confirmed ship losses queued one explosion cue each, including after the result. The queues enforced at least 81ms/141ms between playback calls. Cosmic Forge instead made per-shot/per-impact calls and dropped calls inside its `>80ms`/`>140ms` guards, while this intermediate adapter deferred aggregate proxies. Timer callbacks retried after an early wake. This queue implementation was removed by the event-boundary correction recorded below; this dated note is historical and does not describe current behavior. No browser session was run; see the [battle audio notes](../../../tests/e2e/battle/README.md).

**P-29 battle audio event-boundary correction (12 October 2026, superseded later the same day):** an intermediate update removed deferred queues and attempted one cue at each round/casualty event. That coarse event-boundary approximation was replaced by the ordered aggregate impact/death events below. Its dated details are historical; no test or browser evidence was produced for that intermediate implementation.

**P-29 ordered aggregate battle impact/death events (12 October 2026):** each active fleet-volley now emits a typed impact event with its attacker, target, round, and represented shooter count; damage-caused fleet deaths emit ordered death events, clipped to final casualties. The audio adapter applies one source-style 20% attempt per represented shooter candidate, chooses a cue only after the strict `>80ms` guard admits it, and makes one explosion attempt per confirmed death under the strict `>140ms` guard. Attempts are synchronous, including during catch-up; mute, zero volume, focus loss, or locked audio still advances the guard, with no deferred cues after a result. Combat calculations and engine randomness are unchanged. Aggregate fleets still lack source unit geometry/visibility and exact intra-volley animation timestamps, so candidate count and death timing remain approximations; Power Siphon also heals at a different point and death cues are clipped to final losses. No tests, builds, typechecks, or browser checks were run. P-29 remains open for focused cadence evidence and the documented source-model limits; see the [battle audio notes](../../../tests/e2e/battle/README.md) and [source audit](../../archive/audits/P29-battle-audit-2026-10-11.md).

**P-55 Energy rebirth behavior (12 October 2026):** source rebirth restores plant rates and battery capacities from the initial snapshot and replays Optimized Power Grids. The owner chose source behavior: both mutations are run-scoped in MIAPLACIDUS; `restoreMegastructureProgressOnRebirth` clears both flags even when restoring a reused run object, while permanent Optimized Power Grids remains applied through acquired perks and stage 5 alone restores Infinite Power and the grid. Schema v53 records the plant-rate flag; v54 adds the battery-capacity flag. The v52→v53 and v53→v54 migrations preserve prior live behavior before the next rebirth. **P-55 Cosmic Rip telemetry rate correction (12 October 2026):** Sensor Buoy and Rip Research Orbiter now accrue at 4 and 7 telemetry/s, matching Cosmic Forge's 0.04/0.07 per 10 ms update tick after its 100× per-second conversion. The v52→v53 migration scales saved lifetime-earned history by 100 and reconstructs the current balance as 100 times its old value plus 99 times telemetry costs already paid for completed and active research; earlier saves reach this rung through the migration chain to current schema v54. Pre-v38 earned history is unrecoverable and remains zero, and balance reconstruction is limited by the old eight-decimal rounding. Valid converted balances above `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` are retained; only conversion overflow saturates at `Number.MAX_VALUE`. See the [source-linked rate audit](../../archive/audits/p55-cosmic-rip-telemetry-rate-audit-2026-10-12.md). No tests or checks were run; P-55 remains open.
